## INTRODUCTION

The blockchain is a decentralized and distributed ledger that stores transactions in a transparent and secure manner. The transactions are stored in a series of blocks linked together using the concept of hashing. The information stored on the blockchain is immutable and transparent. These properties of blockchain make it useful in various applications such as financial transactions, supply chain management, healthcare data management, *etc.* Blockchain technology can help transform the healthcare industry in various ways. It can streamline hospital operations and provide secure, transparent management of patient health records. To safeguard the patients’ privacy and security, healthcare data must be protected

due to its highly sensitive nature. Blockchain technology provides secure and private data storage and sharing. It enables us to store patient data in an encrypted, decentralized way. This reduces the chance of a single point of failure and helps prevent security incidents. Another important benefit is that it facilitates interoperability across various healthcare providers. Healthcare data is often siloed and fragmented, making accessing and sharing important information difficult. By offering a safe and transparent platform for data sharing between various healthcare systems, blockchain technology aids in the resolution of this problem. Additionally, it can help lower administrative expenses and improve operations.

Technological developments such as electronic health records (EHRs), wearable devices, and telemedicine have enabled healthcare providers to deliver more efficient and effective patient care. EHRs, for instance, have streamlined the process for healthcare providers to store, retrieve, and share information about patients, leading to improved care coordination and reduced errors. On the other hand, patients are now able to receive medical care remotely with the advent of telemedicine, which is particularly useful for patients in rural or remote areas. Patients can now more easily monitor their health and wellness thanks to wearable technology, such as fitness trackers, which also give medical professionals useful information for preventive care. These technological advancements have also led to significant cost savings and improved patient care, making them a valuable asset to the healthcare industry.

The healthcare industry involves many stakeholders, including healthcare providers, payers, patients, and regulators. It is responsible for delivering the medical services and products essential for diagnosing, treating, and preventing diseases. It is constantly evolving, pushed by improvements in medical technology, changes in patient needs, and shifts in regulations and policies. However, the industry still faces many challenges, such as rising costs, increasing demand, *etc.* As a result, there is a growing need for innovative solutions to improve healthcare services' quality and affordability. Blockchain technology holds the potential to revolutionize the delivery, management, and financing of healthcare, making it a promising solution to the challenges faced by the industry. By enabling secure and transparent patient data management, blockchain technology can improve stakeholder coordination, reduce errors, and enhance patient care. It can also improve the privacy of patient data, protect against cyber threats, and enable interoperability between various healthcare systems and services. As such, healthcare organizations are increasingly exploring and adopting it to address the challenges and opportunities of the digital age.

This paper attempts to present a comprehensive overview of the innovative applications of blockchain in the healthcare industry. We will also explore its potential benefits and examine the challenges. We also present case studies of successful implementations and identify the prospects for using blockchain in healthcare. By delivering a thorough understanding of the applications of blockchain in healthcare, we aim to inform healthcare providers, policymakers, and researchers about the potential of this technology to transform the healthcare sector and enhance patient care.

## LITERATURE REVIEW

Several studies have investigated the possible uses of blockchain technology in healthcare and have identified its benefits and challenges \[1\]. Bittins *et al.* \[[2](#9789815305210-C7.html#r2)\] presented a blockchain-based architecture that aims to improve the trust in existing data transfer solutions built on the IHE technical standards. The authors discuss how blockchain is able to improve trust in medical information-sharing procedures by automating provenance tracking and accountable credential verification, and they situate the latest research findings within the regulatory and technical standard frameworks for healthcare, such as IHE and FHIR. As an inspiring and insightful case study, the data exchange methods associated with international organ transplant operations are used. The authors explain how their blockchain-based architecture incorporates SSI credential verification using the EBSI infrastructure and automatically generated provenance annotations for patient medical data using the W3C PROV standard. The integrity of data interactions among communities is improved by this principled integration. The authors also draw attention to the legal specifications that should be incorporated into the planning and implementation of blockchain-based healthcare services of the future. Incorporating SSI into various healthcare settings can result in services that are more adaptable and convenient for patients. The development of reproducible clinical research can be supported by automatic provenance management, and SSI can open the door to the adoption of the “Once Only Principle” in the healthcare industry. Ciampi *et al.* \[[3](#9789815305210-C7.html#r3)\] explored the possibility and difficulties of incorporating the FHIR standard with distributed ledger technologies (DLTs). The authors mention a longitudinal record and a “smart care plan,” which are concepts outside the purview of EHR systems, as crucial advances in the direction of predictive, preventive, personalized, and participatory (P4-medicine). The authors claim that the certification and verification of clinical events for the advancement of health processes can be accomplished through the integration of FHIR in DLTs. They also provide examples of how blockchain technologies might be utilized to uphold the integrity of workflows linked to FHIR resources and their authentication. The authors conclude that utilizing FHIR for the specific scenario of dynamic care planning, as described by the IHE DCP profile, could serve as an initial step in combining FHIR with DLTs. The significance of blockchain in the healthcare industry extends beyond its functional applications and includes key elements such as performance optimization, security, privacy, and trust management. In a research paper titled “Security, Privacy, Trust Management, and Performance Optimization of Blockchain Technology,” Swarnkar *et al.* (2019) \[[4](#9789815305210-C7.html#r4)\] emphasized the crucial role of building a blockchain system with strong security and privacy features to prevent data breaches, cyber-attacks, network intrusions, and unauthorized data manipulation. The paper presents several recommendations to address privacy, security, and trust concerns in blockchain technology and assesses the impact of enhancing security and privacy on blockchain's performance.

Patient data is a critical part of healthcare, and the interchange of healthcare information is a vital component that helps both patients and health service providers. Gupta *et al.* (2021) \[[5](#9789815305210-C7.html#r5)\] discuss the usage of blockchain to make digital documents and their transfer in the healthcare system safe and encrypted. While current methods mostly concentrate on gathering patient records from medical examinations, the suggested alternative employs blockchain smart contracts to safeguard data transfer. By providing encrypted data, this method prevents data loss and increases the security of the transfer. A digital medical record management system utilizing blockchain technology was suggested by Venkatesan *et al.* (2021) \[[6](#9789815305210-C7.html#r6)\] to leverage the benefits of eHealthcare while protecting the data from possible security risks. The proposed system incorporates immutable logging of access information to meet audits and legal compliance requirements. Notably, issues with the patient's multiple accounts and inaccessibility of the data when the patient is not responding have also been sorted out. To evaluate the overhead and applicability, the suggested system is constructed by altering the go-ethereum implementation.

Abdelgalil and Mejri \[[10](#9789815305210-C7.html#r10)\] proposed HealthBlock, a collaborative framework utilizing blockchain technology to address the challenges of decentralized and heterogeneous electronic health records (EHRs). The framework combines IPFS for distributed storage, Hyperledger Indy for patient control, and Hyperledger Fabric for access control policies. Karmakar *et al.* \[[11](#9789815305210-C7.html#r11)\] introduced ChainSure, an Ethereum blockchain-based framework that combines TOPSIS and smart contracts for automated, tamperproof, and transparent medical insurance. ChainSure's decentralized nature and interactive interface enable users to find suitable policies, outperforming other models in service integrity, latency, and cost. Azbeg *et al.* \[[12](#9789815305210-C7.html#r12)\] proposed BlockMedCare, a secure healthcare system integrating IoT with blockchain to address security issues with IoT-based systems. It employs re-encryption proxy and IPFS for scalable data storage, smart contracts for access management, and blockchain for data security. The system demonstrates improved healthcare system security in the context of diabetes management. According to Ismail and Materwala (2020) \[[13](#9789815305210-C7.html#r13)\], the current EHR management system based on client-server architecture faces challenges related to integrity, interoperability, privacy, and confidentiality. To address these issues, they suggest BlockHR, a blockchain-based framework for managing health records that offers improved data management and healthcare outcomes.

Nishi *et al.* (2023) \[[14](#9789815305210-C7.html#r14)\] proposed a system based on blockchain for managing and securing patients' data, leveraging the Ethereum network and tools like Solidity and web3.js. It utilizes smart contracts to ensure secure transactions and employs a cryptocurrency wallet (MetaMask) for centralized access control. They highlight the benefits of blockchain technology, including increased efficiency, enhanced credibility, reduced barriers, and secure exchange of patient medical records, ultimately promoting openness and ownership of sensitive healthcare data. The study conducted by Singh *et al.* (2023) \[[15](#9789815305210-C7.html#r15)\] provides a thorough analysis of the positive and negative aspects of utilizing blockchain technology for efficient data management in the healthcare system. Yaqoob *et al.* (2023) \[[16](#9789815305210-C7.html#r16)\] claimed that blockchain technology can address the issues that the current healthcare data management systems face, such as data transparency, accountability, and privacy. Xu Cheng *et al.* \[[17](#9789815305210-C7.html#r17)\] proposed a network model based on blockchain for medical cyber-physical systems (MCPS) to ensure secure data sharing in healthcare. Their approach addresses authentication challenges, provides data integrity and traceability, and eliminates the need for a trusted third party. The scheme demonstrates effective security protocols and manageable storage and computing overhead costs, making it suitable for the secure sharing of medical big data. Spanò, Massaro, and Iacuzzi (2021) \[[18](#9789815305210-C7.html#r18)\] investigated the contribution of blockchain technology to create value in the healthcare sector by analyzing 33 companies that are part of a sample consisting of 2404 enterprises founded by professional incubators and accelerators. Through content analysis of whitepapers, websites, and weblog discussions, the study identifies four distinct approaches to value creation. Chelladurai and Pandian \[[19](#9789815305210-C7.html#r19)\] proposed a blockchain solution for exchanging health information and providing patients with easy access to electronic health records across providers. The system ensures secure storage, rapid access, and integrity through cryptographic hash functions, and its effectiveness is evaluated through extensive trials. Verma, Pathak, and Sharma (2019) \[[20](#9789815305210-C7.html#r20)\] proposed a secure framework for medical record management using blockchain in a cloud environment, aiming to accomplish secure access control and privacy of electronic healthcare data (EHD) through the utilization of searchable key attribute-based encryption (ABE). Their simulation shows that the suggested system is both secure and effective.

Luong and Park \[[21](#9789815305210-C7.html#r21)\] addressed the challenging task of simultaneously protecting the privacy of legitimate users and preventing unauthorized access to health services in the smart health industry. By leveraging the public blockchain's anonymity feature and the zk-SNARK as an anonymous authenticator, their proposed healthcare system protects user privacy and prevents unauthorized use. The authors also demonstrated the system's robustness against various attacks and assessed its performance in terms of computational costs and transaction fees in interaction with the Ethereum blockchain smart contract. Kassab *et al.* (2019) \[[22](#9789815305210-C7.html#r22)\] explored the research on the symbiotic relationship between blockchain technology and the healthcare domain, revealing the benefits and challenges of incorporating blockchain while also proposing a framework to guide future research activities. In their systematic review, Merlo *et al.* (2022) \[[23](#9789815305210-C7.html#r23)\] analyzed a collection of publications retrieved from PubMed, Scopus, and Web of Science, with an emphasis on real-world applications. The study revealed that data security and the implementation of electronic health records based on blockchain are the main areas of focus for current research activities. However, areas such as IoT integration and the application of automated diagnosis systems remain relatively under-explored in the healthcare sector. According to Matthias Mettler \[[24](#9789815305210-C7.html#r24)\], the purpose of the report is to provide an overview of the potential impacts, objectives, and opportunities associated with blockchain technology as it relates to the healthcare domain. Khatri *et al.* (2021) \[[25](#9789815305210-C7.html#r25)\] conducted a systematic analysis of 50 papers to examine blockchain integration in healthcare. They identified emerging trends, highlighted challenges, and emphasized the use of privately held blockchain platforms. The study also identified potential developments like blockchain integration with artificial intelligence, solutions that are cloud-based, and parallel blockchain architecture. According to Abu-elezz *et al.* (2020) \[[26](#9789815305210-C7.html#r26)\], blockchain technology can potentially improve data exchange and storage in healthcare through its decentralization, immutability, transparency, and traceability features. However, concerns regarding security and authorization, interoperability challenges, and limited technical expertise in blockchain technology pose significant threats to its widespread adoption in healthcare organizations.

Hussien *et al*. study \[[27](#9789815305210-C7.html#r27)\] used bibliometric analysis to identify the blockchain technology's trend in the healthcare sector. Additionally, they evaluated case studies on telemedicine and E-health, focusing on security and privacy aspects. The study also discussed the advantages and disadvantages of using blockchain technology, such as the exchange of health data, clinical trials, big data, artificial intelligence, 5G ultrasonic devices, the pharmaceutical sector, and privacy and security concerns. According to Hasavari and Song (2019) \[[28](#9789815305210-C7.html#r28)\], the lack of access to critical patient data and delays in reaching hospitals have been identified as contributing factors to the mortality rate in emergency medical care. To address this issue, the authors propose a solution that combines secure file-sharing techniques and blockchain technology, enabling the continuous recording of patient emergency data as they move between healthcare providers. This approach ensures a reliable data source, allowing paramedics to access and utilize the information for high-quality pre-hospital care while addressing concerns regarding authentication, privacy, security, scalability, audibility, and confidentiality. The decentralized healthcare management system proposed by Deepa *et al.* \[[29](#9789815305210-C7.html#r29)\] utilizes blockchain technology to address the lack of stringent authentication and security for patient data in existing healthcare systems, allowing for efficient data interoperability and authentication through the ADS application, which refers to strict authentication and decentralized storage using blockchain.

Overall, the literature implies that blockchain can potentially transform the healthcare industry by addressing the existing challenges related to data security, privacy, and interoperability. Systems based on blockchain provide security, privacy, and trust management, making the transfer of data more secure and preventing data loss. However, the overheads associated with these systems must be analyzed to ensure their applicability.

## APPLICATIONS OF BLOCKCHAIN IN HEALTHCARE

### Provenance-Based Architecture

Blockchain technology can offer a robust solution to health data sharing challenges across isolated communities by ensuring quality assessment of data, certification of data origin, and responsible credential verification. A subfield of data management called provenance gathers, maintains, and retrieves metadata detailing the production, exchange, and updation of data. It is essential for determining data quality since it presents semantically related metadata for source attribution. Widely used as a standard for provenance management, the W3C standard PROV was created by the Web Science community. The first step in determining the source of data is to construct a graph of semantically related concepts that characterize the entities, actions, and agents involved in the business process being examined. Healthcare places a premium on data provenance. For the purpose of providing other system entities with a reliable service, data that is created or modified by a logically stand-alone entity is essential. Blockchain technology can be used to bring transparent provenance management to healthcare systems and be connected with current systems to annotate all transferred medical papers with provenance documents that are written in PROV.

The architecture is designed to integrate with the existing Cross-Enterprise Document Sharing (XDS) system, a standard for sharing medical documents across different healthcare organizations as shown in Fig. (**1**). Provenance materials cannot be related to a patient's medical file unless the document consumer, such as the doctor, has received permission through an XDS patient-informed consent authorization policy. The architecture consists of three main components: the XDS system, the blockchain network, and the provenance proxy. The XDS system is responsible for creating, storing, and sharing medical documents among different healthcare organizations. The blockchain network provides a decentralized infrastructure for storing and managing provenance documents. The Provenance Proxy is a middleware component that intercepts all XDS transactions and generates provenance documents for each transaction. It collects metadata about the document, such as the author, hash, locality, and action performed. The metadata is then used to trigger the generation of a corresponding provenance document stored on the blockchain. The provenance document contains information about the entity that created or modified the document, the time and location of the action, and any other relevant metadata. When a healthcare organization requests a medical document from the XDS system, the provenance proxy intercepts the transaction and retrieves the corresponding provenance document from the blockchain. The provenance document can then be used to verify the authenticity and integrity of the medical document, as well as to trace its lineage and history.

The architecture is designed to be transparent and automated, with no manual intervention required for generating or retrieving provenance documents. It is advised to employ templates to customize provenance annotation to each project's requirements, enabling ontology-based processing and reasoning. The patient-informed consent authorization procedures of XDS are also used to control and regulate the provenance functionalities, protecting patient privacy and confidentiality.

### Integration of FHIR Standard with Blockchain in Healthcare

Distributed ledger technologies (DLTs) and the FHIR standard are two promising avenues for health information management research and development. By combining FHIR and blockchain through Hyperledger Fabric, it is possible to get around EHR system restrictions and build the IT foundation for P4-medicine as shown in Fig. (**2**). The suggested method connects each FHIR server to one or more Fabric peers using a Fabric client that listens in on FHIR server conversations and sends transaction requests over the proper Fabric channel. To ensure the safeguarding of data and processes, it is important to exercise caution in the selection of FHIR resources that will be designated as Fabric assets. Additionally, resource instances must be named and addressed in a manner that avoids name collisions and does not disclose sensitive information, such as patient identifiers. Predictive, preventive, personalized, and participatory medicine may be enabled by this integration.

![](Images/9789815305210-C7-F1.jpg)**Fig. (1))**  
Provenance-based architecture consists of three main components: the XDS system, the blockchain network, and the provenance proxy. The provenance proxy intercepts all XDS transactions and generates provenance documents for each transaction. ![](Images/9789815305210-C7-F2.jpg)**Fig. (2))**  
FHIR and Hyperledger Fabric-based architecture consists of two layers: a network layer implemented using Hyperledger Fabric and an application layer implemented using FHIR. 

The system consists of two layers: a network layer that is implemented using Hyperledger Fabric and an application layer that is implemented using FHIR. A Fabric client serves as an interface between the FHIR and Fabric layers, connecting each FHIR server to one or more Fabric peers. A suitable Fabric channel is used by the Fabric client to send the proper transaction requests after intercepting communications with the FHIR server. The managed FHIR resource determines which Fabric channel is chosen, and the transaction request is based on the nature of the resource interaction.

The relevance of their data and process integrity is taken into consideration when choosing which FHIR resources must be coded as Fabric assets. Given that a resource's full specification can include a number of items, some of which are not required to be recorded, it might be more efficient to keep it there. Replicating resources on the Fabric ledger would add computational and storage costs because the FHIR server already manages and stores them. As a result, only the first few primary FHIR resources are explicitly tracked on the ledger, while the others are dependent upon the first few.

To avoid conflicts with names and the exposure of confidential information, a naming system is employed to recognize and refer to resource instances in both the broader network and a specific server. The suggested method calls for resource names made up of three components: a version number, a globally unique identifier (GUID), and a prefix that is specific to the FHIR server in the network and its service.

The integration of FHIR and Hyperledger Fabric enables the implementation of an intelligent care plan, which is crucial for paving the way toward P4 medicine. Beyond episodic visits, the technology enables all members of the care team to observe and participate in an integrated care plan. It makes it possible to create models for predicting and preventing pandemics and chronic illnesses.

Overall, the proposed system architecture provides a promising solution to the limitations of EHR systems and offers a secure, scalable, and interoperable platform for healthcare data management.

### Integrated Architecture

Healthcare information exchange is crucial for the well-being of patients, and cloud-based solutions are widely used for collecting and sharing patient data. However, the use of external service providers can lead to security concerns. Blockchain technology's use of smart contracts helps safeguard the transfer of healthcare data, such as records from clinical trials, reimbursements, and test findings. This chapter presents a method for creating intelligent representations of medical records that are present in different network nodes using smart contracts. The smart contracts contain data on record ownership, metadata, permissions, and data veracity. Only legitimate transactions that alter the data are processed by state-transition functions in contracts to carry out actions. The blockchain transactions also include cryptographically signed instructions that specify how particular characteristics should be handled. Such rules may be created to control particular medical records, requiring the submission of various consent forms from patients and healthcare providers before allowing third-party access. The proposed model enables clinicians to access patients' medical histories and any ongoing drugs or treatments by using Aadhar card verification in conjunction with smart contracts to validate identity. The network's block content indicates the ownership and access permissions of the data that were traded among multiple participants in a private peer-to-peer network, preserving the integrity of the data.

The proposed architecture aims to utilize blockchain technology, specifically smart contracts, to secure and encrypt the transfer of healthcare data as shown in Fig. (**3**). The authors suggest a system for identity verification that combines smart contracts and Aadhar card verification. Doctors can access patients' medical histories, present medications, and ongoing therapies with the use of this identity verification.

The proposed model consists of the following components:

#### Blockchain Network

A decentralized network where each node stores smart contracts representing medical records. The blockchain is used to store these smart contracts, which allow for the creation of policies that govern access to medical records.

#### Aadhar Card Verification

Aadhar card is a unique identity card issued by the Indian government. The proposed solution verifies a person's identity using their Aadhar card along with smart contracts. Doctors may access patients' medical histories, along with any current drugs or continuing treatments, thanks to this identity verification.

#### Smart Contracts

The proposed model uses smart contracts to store medical records. The smart contracts include policies that govern access to medical records.

#### Data Storage

All information related to a medical record is saved locally in databases and is represented by its hash, which is part of the blockchain block that is tied to the chain. This ensures consistency and economic viability.

![](Images/9789815305210-C7-F3.jpg)**Fig. (3))**  
Integrated architecture proposes a system for identity verification that combines smart contracts and Aadhar card verification. 

#### Syncing Algorithm

A synchronization mechanism controls an “off-chain” communication between patient and provider databases. The data will be exchanged when we use our database authentication service to refer to the blockchain to verify permissions.

The proposed model also features a framework that ties the current and healthcare systems together. Patients may request that records be shared between doctors, and providers may attach additional records to a specific patient. In both situations, the person receiving new data is notified automatically and has the option to review the record prior to either accepting or rejecting the information. This helps participants make decisions and retain ownership over their data by keeping them informed and interested in how their data is evolving.

Overall, the proposed architecture aims to create a secure and encrypted transfer of healthcare data using blockchain technology and smart contracts. It utilizes Aadhar card verification, smart contracts, data storage, and syncing algorithms to achieve this goal.

## BENEFITS OF BLOCKCHAIN IN HEALTHCARE

Blockchain technology has several benefits that can positively impact the healthcare industry. Some of the significant advantages of using blockchain technology in healthcare are:

### Data Security and Privacy

Data security and privacy are serious concerns in the healthcare industry. Healthcare organizations store large volumes of sensitive patient data that must be kept secure and confidential. With the rise of cyber threats, ensuring data security and privacy has become increasingly challenging. High data security is one of the main advantages of using blockchain in the healthcare industry. The decentralized structure of blockchain eliminates the possibility of a single point of failure; thus, even in the event that one node fails, the system keeps running normally. The distributed architecture of the blockchain makes it nearly impossible for hackers to breach the system and access sensitive patient data. Moreover, using advanced cryptography and consensus algorithms ensures that authorized participants have access to the data, making the system more secure. In addition to data security, blockchain technology also enhances privacy in healthcare. The blockchain gives patients full control over their data, deciding who has access to their health records and when. Patients can securely share their medical records with their healthcare providers, ensuring their data remains confidential. Furthermore, smart contracts enable patients to specify the terms and conditions for accessing their data, ensuring their privacy is maintained. The data is more reliable because of the immutable nature of the blockchain, which guarantees that it cannot be tampered with. It ensures that the data remains accurate and trustworthy, making it more valuable for research and clinical purposes.

### Interoperability

Interoperability is a critical challenge in the healthcare industry due to the use of multiple systems and data standards that do not communicate with each other. This results in fragmented and inefficient healthcare delivery, causing patients to suffer from poor quality care and outcomes. By facilitating information sharing among different healthcare providers, blockchain technology implementation in the healthcare industry fosters interoperability. Blockchain technology creates a secure and transparent environment where all healthcare professionals may access and share patient data despite the system or data standard they use. By ensuring that every healthcare provider has access to the same information, it minimizes the possibility of errors and enhances the quality of patient care. Blockchain technology also enhances patient engagement and involvement in their healthcare. With blockchain, patients can access and control their data, making them active participants in their care. Patients can share their healthcare data with providers, enabling personalized care delivery and better health outcomes.

### Efficiency and Cost Reduction

The healthcare industry is known for its complex and often fragmented systems, leading to slow and expensive processes. However, blockchain technology can address these issues and create a more efficient and cost-effective healthcare system. One of the primary ways blockchain technology can improve efficiency is by reducing the need for intermediaries. Intermediaries are individuals or organizations facilitating transactions between healthcare providers, patients, and insurance companies. These intermediaries include third-party administrators, insurers, and billing companies. By utilizing blockchain technology, healthcare providers can eliminate the need for intermediaries, thereby reducing costs and improving the efficiency of transactions. Blockchain technology also enables faster payments. Healthcare providers may need to wait weeks or even months to receive reimbursements from insurance companies or government programs. With blockchain technology, payments can be made in real time, enabling healthcare providers to receive payments faster and improve their cash flow. Another area where blockchain technology can improve healthcare efficiency is by reducing administrative costs. The technology can automate many administrative tasks, such as record-keeping, claim processing, and billing. This automation can significantly minimize administrative expenses and improve the accuracy and speed of these processes.

## CHALLENGES AND LIMITATIONS OF BLOCKCHAIN IN HEALTHCARE

Despite the potential advantages of blockchain technology for healthcare, several difficulties and limitations prevent its widespread use. Some of these challenges include:

### Regulatory and Legal Issues

The strict laws and rules protecting patient safety and privacy have made the healthcare sector highly regulated. There are concerns regarding legal compliance when blockchain technology is used in the healthcare industry. Healthcare providers must follow these regulations to protect the privacy, accuracy, and accessibility of patient data. Blockchain technology can help achieve these goals by providing secure storage of patient data, but it must comply with these regulations to be adopted widely. Another legal issue related to the usage of blockchain technology in healthcare is data ownership, consent, and liability. Healthcare data is a valuable asset, and the ownership of patient data is not always clear. Blockchain technology offers an immutable record of data ownership, but it must also comply with regulations that govern patient consent and data protection. Liability is another legal issue that needs to be addressed when using blockchain technology in healthcare. If patient data is compromised or lost due to a system based on blockchain, who is liable for the damages? These issues must be sorted out before blockchain technology can be widely adopted in healthcare.

### Integration with Legacy Systems

The healthcare industry has a vast network of legacy systems that have been in use for years and are not designed to work with blockchain technology. These systems are often complex, outdated, and incompatible, making it difficult to integrate them with the blockchain network seamlessly. The integration of blockchain technology with legacy systems is essential for creating a secure, transparent, and efficient healthcare ecosystem. However, the integration process requires significant investment in terms of time, money, and resources. The healthcare industry needs to find a way to bridge the gap between these systems and the blockchain network to leverage the benefits of blockchain technology. Another significant challenge is the lack of standardization across different healthcare systems. A unified system is challenging to develop since healthcare providers employ several technologies to store and manage patient data. The lack of standardization across systems poses a significant challenge to the adoption of blockchain technology. Healthcare providers need to develop a common standard that can be adopted across different systems to ensure compatibility with blockchain technology.

### Scalability and Performance

Blockchain technology is designed to ensure the security and immutability of data by decentralizing it and storing it on a distributed ledger. However, as the number of nodes on the network increases, the system's performance can decrease, making it challenging to handle large volumes of data. One of the reasons for this is the way blockchain transactions are verified and added to the blockchain. Each transaction requires a certain amount of computational power to solve complex mathematical algorithms, which takes time and requires significant resources. As the number of transactions increases, the time required to verify and add them to the blockchain also increases, leading to longer processing times and delays in accessing patient information. Another issue related to scalability is the size of the blockchain itself. The size of the database grows as more data is submitted to the blockchain, making it harder to store, access, and share. This can limit the number of nodes participating in the network, leading to longer processing times and poor system performance. While various solutions have been proposed to address these issues, further research and development are needed to ensure that blockchain can meet the demands of the healthcare industry.

## CASE STUDIES

Several organizations have implemented blockchain technology in healthcare to address various challenges in the industry. Some of the notable case studies are:

### MedicalChain

A blockchain-based platform known as MedicalChain \[[7](#9789815305210-C7.html#r7)\] enables users to securely access and manage their medical records and share them with healthcare providers. Only those with permission can access patient data, according to the platform's security measures. The platform's main goal is to provide patients with effective means to manage their medical records. With MedicalChain, patients can control their medical records, share them with healthcare providers, and grant permission to access specific records. As a result, patients are better equipped to take responsibility for their health, more effectively communicate with doctors, and make better decisions.

MedicalChain uses a permissioned blockchain, which means that patient data is only accessible to those who are authorized to do so. The platform encrypts patient data and stores it on the blockchain, ensuring that it is secure and cannot be tampered with. Additionally, the platform makes sure that patient data complies with current laws, including the General Data Protection Regulation (GDPR).

A notable feature of the platform is the telemedicine feature, which enables patients to access healthcare services remotely. Patients can book appointments, consult with doctors, and receive medical advice through the platform's telemedicine feature. The platform also enables patients to receive prescriptions and medical certificates online, eliminating the need to visit a healthcare provider physically.

MedicalChain has partnered with several healthcare providers and institutions worldwide to implement its platform. Some of its notable partners include The Groves Medical Group, The Mayo Clinic, and The Ameri Clerkships Medical Society. These partnerships have enabled MedicalChain to expand its platform's reach and improve healthcare delivery globally.

In conclusion, MedicalChain aims to provide patients with control over their medical records and improve healthcare delivery. The platform's use of blockchain technology ensures that patient data is secure, compliant with regulations, and only accessible to authorized parties. The platform's telemedicine feature and partnerships with healthcare providers worldwide make it a valuable addition to the healthcare industry.

### Solve.Care

Solve.Care \[[8](#9789815305210-C7.html#r8)\] is a platform based on blockchain that aims to revolutionize healthcare delivery by enabling patients, healthcare providers, and payers to interact more efficiently. It aims to simplify healthcare administration by providing a platform that automates many of the processes involved in healthcare delivery. The platform uses blockchain technology to ensure that patient data is secure, transparent, and only accessible to those who have been granted permission. It also uses smart contracts to facilitate healthcare payments, reducing administrative costs and improving payment efficiency. Patients can manage their healthcare needs with the Care.Wallet smartphone app, which is one of the noteworthy features of the platform. It enables them to book appointments, receive reminders, and communicate with healthcare providers. The Care.Wallet also enables patients to receive personalized care plans, making it easier for them to manage their health. Solve.Care also provides a platform for healthcare providers and payers to interact more efficiently. The platform enables healthcare providers to manage patient care more effectively, reducing administrative costs and improving patient outcomes. The platform also provides payers with greater transparency and control over healthcare payments, reducing fraud and improving payment accuracy.

Solve.Care has partnered with several healthcare providers, payers, and institutions worldwide to implement its platform. Some of its notable partners include Arizona Care Network, Uber Health, and BCBS of Arizona. These partnerships have enabled Solve.Care to expand its platform's reach and improve healthcare delivery globally.

In conclusion, Solve.Care aims to simplify healthcare administration and improve healthcare delivery. By using blockchain technology and smart contracts, the platform ensures that patient data is secure, transparent, and only accessible to authorized parties. The Care.Wallet and partnerships with healthcare providers and payers worldwide make it a valuable addition to the healthcare industry.

### MediLedger

MediLedger \[[9](#9789815305210-C7.html#r9)\] is a blockchain-based platform designed to enhance the supply chain efficiency and security of the pharmaceutical industry. The platform was launched in 2017 by Chronicled, a San Francisco-based blockchain technology company. It uses blockchain technology to provide a secure, transparent, and decentralized system for tracking and verifying pharmaceutical products throughout the supply chain.

MediLedger's main goal is to prevent counterfeit drugs from entering the market by creating a system that tracks each drug's journey from manufacturer to the patient. This system makes it easier to validate the authenticity of drugs, reduces the chances of counterfeit drugs entering the supply chain, and increases patient safety.

The platform also enables supply chain participants to manage product recalls and prevent drug shortages. MediLedger provides a more efficient system for managing recalls, making it easier to identify affected products and remove them from the market. The platform also enables stakeholders to identify shortages and take action to prevent them, ensuring that patients have access to the medication they need.

MediLedger uses a permissioned blockchain, which means that access to the platform is restricted to authorized parties. This ensures that patient data is secure and only accessible to those who have been granted permission. The platform also uses smart contracts to automate and streamline supply chain processes, reducing administrative costs and improving efficiency.

MediLedger has several notable partnerships within the pharmaceutical industry. In 2019, MediLedger was chosen as a pilot project by the US Food and Drug Administration (FDA) to explore blockchain technology's potential to enhance the drug supply chain's security and transparency. MediLedger has also partnered with several major pharmaceutical companies, including Pfizer, Merck, and Genentech, to develop and implement blockchain-based solutions for the pharmaceutical supply chain.

In conclusion, MediLedger is a platform with great potential to improve the safety and effectiveness of the pharmaceutical supply chain. By utilizing blockchain technology, the platform guarantees that patient data is secure, visible, and accessible only to authorized entities. The platform's partnerships with major pharmaceutical companies and regulatory agencies make it a valuable addition to the pharmaceutical industry.

## FUTURE DIRECTIONS

While blockchain adoption in the healthcare sector is still in its early stages, there is significant potential for development and impact. The use of blockchain technology in healthcare may advance in these areas in the future:

### Interoperability

Interoperability refers to the capability of different systems to interact with each other and seamlessly exchange information. In the healthcare domain, interoperability is essential for better patient care and patient outcomes. However, the healthcare industry's need for interoperability has been a persistent challenge. Blockchain technology can help to overcome this challenge by creating a decentralized, secure, and transparent system for sharing data between different systems. Blockchain technology allows for creating a shared ledger that can be accessed by different parties while also ensuring the security and privacy of the data. With the use of blockchain technology, healthcare providers can share patient information across different systems without the need for intermediaries, reducing administrative burdens and improving patient outcomes. Additionally, patients can have more control over their data with the use of blockchain technology, which enables them to choose who has access to their health information.

### Standardization

The lack of standardization in healthcare has been a significant barrier to the adoption of new technologies and systems. The lack of standardization makes integrating different systems and technologies challenging, leading to increased costs and complexity. Blockchain technology has the potential to create standardized protocols for data sharing and storage, reducing the complexity and cost of implementing new healthcare technologies and systems. By using blockchain technology, healthcare providers can create a shared platform that is accessible to all stakeholders in the healthcare industry, including patients, healthcare providers, and insurance companies. This shared platform can provide standardized protocols for data sharing and storage, ensuring that data is shared and stored in a reliable and safe way. This can reduce costs, improve efficiency, and improve patient outcomes.

### Regulatory Frameworks

To ensure patient privacy and safety when blockchain technology is implemented in healthcare, new regulatory frameworks must be created. Regulatory bodies will need to work closely with the healthcare industry to develop these frameworks to ensure blockchain technology is used effectively and responsibly. The regulatory frameworks will need to handle issues like data privacy, security, confidentiality, and standards for data sharing and storage. Additionally, regulatory frameworks will need to address issues related to liability, such as who is responsible in the event of a patient data breach.

### Expansion of Use Cases

There is still a lot to be explored about the potential uses of blockchain technology in the healthcare industry. It is likely that more innovative and practical applications of blockchain technology will be discovered as it advances, leading to increased efficiency and improved healthcare outcomes.

## CONCLUSION

In conclusion, the healthcare sector can undergo a transformation through the adoption of blockchain technology, which can lead to increased efficiency, reduced expenses, and better patient outcomes. A system for managing and exchanging healthcare information between various parties can be established through blockchain technology, which offers security, transparency, and decentralization. This survey paper has discussed several applications of blockchain technology in healthcare, including Medicalchain, Solve.Care, and MediLedger. These applications demonstrate blockchain technology’s potential to improve the efficiency and security of the healthcare industry, providing patients with better outcomes and experiences. Furthermore, the paper has highlighted some advantages of using blockchain technology in healthcare, including increased security, transparency, and accessibility. However, there are also drawbacks and challenges associated with using blockchain technology in healthcare, such as lack of standardization and regulatory concerns. Although the use of blockchain technology in healthcare is still in its early stages, the potential for growth and impact is significant. As blockchain technology develops and matures, we will likely see more innovative applications in the healthcare industry that will improve patient outcomes and experiences.

# Blockchain-Powered Monitoring of Healthcare Credentials through Blockchain-Based Technology

  
  
**Rahul Joshi^([1](#9789815305210-C8.html#aff1), [\*](#9789815305210-C8.html#cor1))****,** **Shashi Kant Gupta^([2](#9789815305210-C8.html#aff2))****,** **Rajesh Natarajan^([3](#9789815305210-C8.html#aff3))****,** **Krishna Pandey^([1](#9789815305210-C8.html#aff1))****,** **Suman Kumari^([1](#9789815305210-C8.html#aff1))**  
¹ Department of Journalism & Mass Communication, SMeH, Manav Rachna International Institute of Research & Studies, Faridabad, Haryana, India  
² Computer Science and Engineering, Eudoxia Research University, New Castle, USA  
³ Department of Information Technology, University of Technology and Applied Sciences, Shinas, Oman  
  

## Abstract

The healthcare industry is using the Internet of Things (IoT) extensively. The healthcare sector manages sensitive information, including an individual’s medical history, blood pressure, and other relevant data. Consequently, the technologies used in this domain are becoming more susceptible to attacks because of their heightened sensitivity. Therefore, it is essential to protect the data. Blockchain technology has been shown to provide substantial benefits in achieving this goal. Incorporating blockchain technology into IoT devices has yielded substantial benefits, thanks to notable technical progress. This chapter thoroughly examines the characteristics of blockchain technology that enhance its efficiency in managing sensitive data and ensuring data security and privacy. Furthermore, this chapter systematically explains the region’s many security obstacles and how blockchain technology might successfully overcome them. The study’s results indicate that future research should explore how gender, age, and knowledge of blockchains affect the adoption of blockchain technology in innovative healthcare systems. An alternate field of research involves evaluating the many elements that influence the adoption of the technology known as blockchain.

**Keywords:** AI, Adoption, Blockchain, Clinical, Deep learning, Electronic health record, fraud, IoT, Interoperability, Medical supplies, Monitoring, Research, Smart contracts, Privacy, Pos, Patient, Security, Technology, Tracing, Trail.

## INTRODUCTION

The emergence of blockchain technology and its core features of decentralization, transparency, and pseudonymity coincided with the introduction of the Bitcoin cryptocurrency in 2008 \[[1](#9789815305210-C8.html#r1)\]. With around 400 million transactions successfully

executed as of March 19, 2019 \[[2](#9789815305210-C8.html#r2)\], Bitcoin is a convincing illustration of how blockchain technology may be used. The substantial discussion and proposal of using blockchain technology in data-intensive areas, such as healthcare, has been well-documented \[[3](#9789815305210-C8.html#r3)\]. IBM’s study found that 70% of healthcare professionals expect blockchain technology to impact the health sector significantly. This impact would be seen in areas such as enhancing regulatory compliance, managing clinical trials, and creating a decentralized platform for exchanging electronic health records \[[4](#9789815305210-C8.html#r4)\]. Moreover, the market worth of blockchain applications in the healthcare industry is estimated to exceed $900 million by 2024. While there is a general belief that blockchain has the potential to enhance healthcare IT, the existing study offers only a restricted comprehension of the designed, evaluated, or implemented applications. Moreover, the current enthusiasm around this technology has fostered notions and tactics that may be more practical and well-founded. When assessing the suitability of blockchain technology in the healthcare industry, which encompasses healthcare, biological medicine, and medical education, it is essential to evaluate if existing research correlates with expected results. Moreover, it has been used in financial services, logistics, energy, commodities trading, healthcare, and more. Using blockchain technology, a safe and immutable system may be established to track the origin and path of medicines, aiding in the battle against the widespread distribution of counterfeit drugs. Blockchain-based distributed shared data systems enable several participants in the supply chain to store and exchange transaction records securely. The use of cryptographic techniques enables the restriction of ledger access to authorized entities while preventing unauthorized entities from gaining entry. It offers a method to prevent the dissemination of counterfeit pharmaceuticals across the pharmaceutical supply chain. This approach oversees and regulates the distribution of fraudulent pharmaceuticals. This chapter analyzes the use of blockchain technology in medication monitoring, examines its advantages and disadvantages, and studies several possibilities for protecting the supply chain of drugs.

## HISTORICAL BACKGROUND OF BLOCKCHAIN

Blockchain is a distributed and unchangeable record that keeps track of transactions. It obviates the need for a reliable intermediary to facilitate communication between parties. The blockchain is a type of decentralized digital ledger that maintains an expanding information collection, known as “blocks”, sequentially. Once these blocks are included in the blockchain, cryptographic techniques establish connections between them and the preceding and subsequent blocks \[[5](#9789815305210-C8.html#r5)\]. When adequately executed, blockchain enables universal access to reading, writing, and verifying the integrity of a data block. Due to these characteristics, blockchain is being contemplated for various applications. Blockchain technology enables the implementation of smart contracts, eliminating the need for third-party oversight \[[6](#9789815305210-C8.html#r6)\]. Bitcoin and other cryptocurrencies pioneered a revolutionary technology known as blockchain in 2008-2009 \[[7](#9789815305210-C8.html#r7)\]. It offers various operations in banking, healthcare, transportation, and government, among other areas. This contemporary advancement allows us to meticulously monitor our belongings methodically and securely, beyond any previous methods \[[8](#9789815305210-C8.html#r8), [9](#9789815305210-C8.html#r9)\].

## INTEGRATE IOT DEVICES WITH BLOCKCHAIN

The secure management of real-time, irrefutable patient health metrics and surrounding environmental factors essential to the effective delivery of healthcare is transformed through the integration of IoT devices with blockchain technology. Wearable health monitors, smart medical devices, and environmental sensors are examples of Internet of Things (IoT) devices that collect a variety and volume of data from patient vital signs and physical activity to medication adherence and air quality and temperature in the surroundings. This information is necessary to be able to offer treatment quickly and effectively. Due to the magic of blockchain technology, this data can be documented and stored on a distributed and immutable ledger, making it untouchable and impossible to tamper with. Now, when IoT data are stored on the blockchain, they are securely authenticated because of the cryptographic hashing, and no unauthorized party is allowed to access these data, owing to the consensus processes *e.g.*, Proof of Authority or Practical Byzantine Fault Tolerance that underpin the blockchain network. Combining IoT and blockchain technologies facilitates real-time data mining with the safekeeping of patient information, making it transparent and tamper-proof, as well as the dependability and precision of patient information, thereby empowering healthcare providers to make evidence-based decisions and deliver more personalized therapies. This will allow patients a higher level of abstraction of data to be more securely shared and maintained, reducing privacy concerns and building trust. Second, by gathering data on the environment, it is possible to better understand and combat environmental health factors, such as pollution or extreme temperatures, and provide full care service. It also directly relates to the betterment of patients, increased data security, and better functioning of healthcare processes.

## INCREASING IMPORTANCE OF BLOCKCHAIN FOR HEALTHCARE

The healthcare business increasingly relies on assistance from other areas, such as computer science, which may significantly contribute to this domain \[[10](#9789815305210-C8.html#r10)\]. This encompasses a wide range of fields, such as genomics, gene prediction, electronic health records, and the development of disease diagnostic tools \[[11](#9789815305210-C8.html#r11)\]. Public health organizations and commercial businesses possess advanced systems that routinely store significant quantities of patient data. This brings up the issue of efficiently overseeing and using this enormous amount of information. Recently, the concept of “big data analysis” has become more popular to tackle this challenge. According to Lee & Yoon (2017), “Big data analysis” encompasses specialized techniques to analyze extensive and intricate datasets that cannot be efficiently processed using conventional data analysis methods \[[12](#9789815305210-C8.html#r12)\].

The use of big data in medicine has the potential to bring about advancements in healthcare diagnostic prediction, MRI analysis, and several other domains \[[13](#9789815305210-C8.html#r13)\]. Computer science, being multidisciplinary, may be used in several fields of study to enhance and optimize the corresponding procedures. The medical industry may use computers and associated technologies to address various issues. Healthcare systems may see improvements in quality, efficiency, and cost reduction \[[14](#9789815305210-C8.html#r14)\].

## ASPECTS OF BLOCKCHAIN TO SUPPORT UNIVERSAL MEDICAL INDUSTRY

Ledger technology facilitates the identification of genetic codes by managing the supply chain of medications, ensuring secure transmission of patient medical information, and streamlining safe transfer processes. The technical features of blockchain technology include safeguarding healthcare data, managing genomes, handling electronic data, maintaining medical records, ensuring interoperability, monitoring digitalized information, and addressing epidemics, among other capabilities. The growth of blockchain technology in healthcare applications is driven by its digital character. The blockchain enables the traceability of prescriptions along the whole supply chain, from the manufacturing process to the availability on pharmacy shelves. We can observe and track traffic congestion, commodities flow, and movement velocity using IoT and Blockchain. It enables the efficient coordination of procurement to mitigate interruptions and shortages in pharmacy chains, clinics, and other healthcare facilities that depend on a specific prescription. Utilizing blockchain technology effectively deters unauthorized alterations to logistical data. It enhances self-assurance and safeguards drug purchasers from any mistreatment of data, finances, and medication. Technology can improve patient conditions at a reasonable cost while maintaining financial stability. It eliminates all obstacles related to multi-level authentication \[[15](#9789815305210-C8.html#r15)\]. Blockchain is very suitable for security applications because it can securely and transparently store patient data without the risk of corruption. Blockchain technology ensures visibility and privacy by using intricate and secure algorithms to safeguard individuals’ identities in medical data transactions. The decentralization of the technology enables swift and secure information communication among patients, doctors, and healthcare professionals.

Blockchain innovation has several medical uses, as seen in Fig. ([**1**](#9789815305210-C8.html#f9.1)). Blockchain technology facilitates the shift to interoperability by enabling patients to make their medical data readily available and comply with access regulations. This enhances patient autonomy in managing personal information and the levels of secrecy and privacy. Measuring and implementing quality management and enforcement pose significant challenges. Blockchain applications can resolve any of these technological difficulties throughout the sector. Blockchain can regulatory agencies in tracking genuine pharmaceuticals compared to counterfeit ones. This guarantees that all authorized entities exchange digital transactions, including patients’ information. Patients who change healthcare providers may request permission to transfer their medical records” \[[16](#9789815305210-C8.html#r16)\]. The healthcare business has increasingly embraced blockchain technology.

![](Images/9789815305210-C8-F1.jpg)**Fig. (1))**  
Utilization of blockchain technology in the medical sector. 

Furthermore, during the first phases, individuals within the health ecosystem readily embrace the innovations. In the future, Blockchain’s comprehensive perspective will aim to resolve issues that now impact the healthcare industry. It enables doctors, patients, and chemists to get all the accessible information at a particular time. Medical companies are actively researching and testing blockchain technology to manage health information in the medical profession. The healthcare industry has established itself as an essential tool by adhering to pharmaceutical practices, enhancing payment alternatives, and dispersing patient health information. Blockchain, machine learning, and AI are crucial to the medical industry. Blockchain’s medical transformation has valid uses. The program utilizes blockchain monitoring technologies to optimize the medical supply chain \[[17](#9789815305210-C8.html#r17)\]. “Blockchain is an advanced data storage system that securely stores an individual’s health history, including diagnoses, test results, previous treatments, and even readings from intelligent sensors. This strategy allows a clinician to get all the relevant information efficiently to make accurate diagnoses and recommendations. Due to the comprehensive storage of data inside a singular Blockchain system, it is impervious to loss and alteration.” An entity may use blockchain to bypass its internal networks. An intricate network of several entities, each with different degrees of authority, operating on a secure blockchain database has the potential to protect businesses from external threats and attacks. Implementing a blockchain network in a healthcare organization may minimize rescue assaults and other issues like computer corruption or hardware failure \[[18](#9789815305210-C8.html#r18)\].

## HEALTHCARE BENEFITS OF BLOCKCHAIN-BASED TECHNOLOGY

Blockchain technology has several advantages in healthcare, tackling diverse obstacles and enhancing medical systems’ overall effectiveness, safety, and openness. Fig. ([**2**](#9789815305210-C8.html#f9.2)) highlights blockchain’s medical advantages.

Several notable advantages involve the following:

### Ease of Access

Individuals using applications equipped with blockchain technology may securely, conveniently, and efficiently retrieve their medical data.

### The Ability to Interoperate

Traditional approaches rely on a centralized data storage system, which adds to the difficulties in achieving seamless compatibility across patients’ records. These problems may be avoided due to the need for a centralized database in blockchain technology. Moreover, all nodes can interact with each other.

### Proof of Identity

The blockchain’s block-based authentication of data scheme uses a distinct private key combined with a publicly accessible key.

![](Images/9789815305210-C8-F2.jpg)**Fig. (2))**  
Benefits of blockchain in the healthcare sector. 

### Decentralzsed Repository

The decentralized structure of blockchain makes it well-suited for storing vast quantities of data across different places. Decentralized storage enables enhanced data quality, expedited medical data retrieval, and elevated security levels.

### Cost Saving

Implementing blockchain technology in the healthcare sector might substantially decrease operating expenses by optimizing administrative processes, improving compatibility across different systems, and facilitating expedited data transmission. Using blockchain technology, healthcare providers may streamline several labor-intensive and paper-dependent procedures in the healthcare industry, including exchanging medical records, managing medication supply chains, and processing insurance claims. This leads to significant reductions in expenses. Moreover, blockchain technology has the potential to provide real-time and precise information on the transportation and distribution of drugs and medical supplies. Improved transparency in the supply chain system reduces the probability of counterfeit products penetrating, enhancing effectiveness and reducing losses caused by counterfeit items.

## NETWORKS OF BLOCKCHAIN TECHNOLOGY

Blockchain may be categorized into several groups, each with its unique traits, and their activities reflect the overall behavior of the network. Alhadhrami *et al.* (2017) argued that these blockchains may be divided into three major categories \[[19](#9789815305210-C8.html#r19)\].

### Public Blockchain

A public blockchain is distinguished by its inclusiveness, enabling unfettered participation and active involvement from any person. This indicates that people from diverse backgrounds can engage as users or miners and that anybody may contribute new blocks. This ensures the existence of transparency in public blockchain networks. A public blockchain, also known as a permissionless blockchain, allows anybody to get a copy of the blockchain and participate in the validation of blocks. Bitcoin and Ethereum exemplify the concept of a public blockchain.

### Private Blockchain

A private blockchain is a limited blockchain that is suitable for specific organizations. An organization establishes the criteria for determining who can access and maintain a shared ledger. Hyperledger is an example of a private blockchain.

### Consortium Blockchain

In blockchain technology, a consortium blockchain is a kind of blockchain maintained by many organizations that needs permission to access. By combining private blockchain technology’s benefits with the transparency typical of a public blockchain, the consortium blockchain offers a unique combination of both. A hybrid blockchain is another name for a consortium blockchain. Dragonchain is an example of a consortium blockchain.

## THE METHOD FOR VERIFYING BLOCKCHAIN

The blockchain validation mechanism, commonly called mining, is recognized by specific algorithms, such as proof of work. This function is executed using a consensus technique, establishing the rules that nodes must adhere to verify the blocks. The consensus process ensures that all validating nodes will obtain a favorable outcome by following the correct sequence of transactions. The decision to include the block in the chain is at their discretion \[[20](#9789815305210-C8.html#r20)\]. Consensus mechanisms are specially designed to authenticate blocks on a blockchain, enhancing the

network’s security. Below is a compilation of the vital healthcare protocols about blockchain that have been released so far.

### Understanding Practical Byzantine Fault Tolerance PBFT in Blockchain

The network’s nodes may be categorized into two distinct groups: clients and servers. The client node establishes a connection with a server node by sending a request. The server node then disseminates the transaction to the other server nodes. Once a server node accepts the transaction, it tells the other server nodes of its readiness to execute the transaction. The PBFT uses this technique to authenticate the transactions. Castro & Liskov (1999) stated that for an interaction to be deemed verified, it must satisfy three conditions \[[21](#9789815305210-C8.html#r21)\]:

1\. It must be confirmed by many nodes, which is also known as a “Confirmation Alert”,

2\. Each validated node must broadcast a message to the network confirming its action,

3\. The sender node must get a response.

### Proof-of-Stake (PoS) in Blockchain

Proof-of-stake decreases the computational workload needed to verify blocks and transactions. Through proof-of-work, the integrity and safety of the blockchain were effectively maintained. Proof-of-stake modifies the block verification process by using the machines possessed by currency holders, hence reducing the computational effort needed. Owners provide their currencies as collateral to verify blocks and take on the role of validators. Validators are selected at random to authenticate transactions and validate block data. This method employs a non-competitive reward-based approach, such as proof-of-work, to determine who will earn fees. A coin owner must “stake” certain coins to become a validator. For example, a user must stake a minimum of 32 ETH to become a validator on the Ethereum network. Blocks undergo validation by many validators, and upon receiving verification from a certain number of validators, the block is deemed accurate, finalized, and closed. Various proof procedures use different approaches to attain agreement. Nodes in the network participate in the process of verifying new blocks and earning fees by staking a certain amount of bitcoin. The method then chooses a node from the candidate pool to authenticate the new block. This selection mechanism uses the deposit quantity of cryptocurrencies in conjunction with other parameters, such as coin age and a randomization procedure, to ensure fairness for all participants in the network \[[22](#9789815305210-C8.html#r22)\].

### Understanding Proof-of-work (PoW) in Blockchain Technology

“Proof of work is a consensus process determining which network users, known as miners, are authorized to undertake the profitable duty of validating new data. The mining process is profitable because the miners are incentivized with newly generated cryptocurrency to properly verify fresh data without engaging in fraudulent activities. Proof of work is a software algorithm employed by Bitcoin and other blockchains to verify the validity of blocks based on the condition that they necessitate a specific level of computational power for their creation,” explains Amaury Sechet, the creator of the cryptocurrency e-Cash. “It is a consensus mechanism that enables anonymous entities in decentralized networks to have trust in each other.” The term “work” in the concept of evidence of work is crucial. The system mandates that miners engage in a competitive race to answer complex mathematical riddles to deter any attempts to manipulate the system. The victor of this race is chosen to append the most recent data or transactions to the blockchain. The reward for winning miners is contingent upon verifying the accuracy and validity of the data added to the chain by other participants in the network \[[23](#9789815305210-C8.html#r23)\].

### Understanding Proof-of-Weight in Blockchain

Proof-of-weight consensus incorporates the concept of “weight” into the fundamental idea of PoW, as determined by algorithm consensus. The weights are directly proportional to the values produced by the nodes in the network. The objective is to prevent “double-spending,” in which a customer might use the same digital token twice by manipulating its relative weight \[[24](#9789815305210-C8.html#r24)\].

### Proof-of-Existence (POE) in Blockchain

This method is designed to verify the existence of documents at a particular time. Data ownership information could be disclosed without revealing the data. The POE paradigm provides evidence of intellectual property papers, such as patents \[[25](#9789815305210-C8.html#r25)\].

## DATA REGARDING BLOCKCHAIN-BASED MONITORING

The issue of fraudulent or counterfeit drugs is growing fast within the healthcare industry. The use of such counterfeit pharmaceuticals could endanger the patient’s well-being. The proliferation of these counterfeit pharmaceuticals \[[26](#9789815305210-C8.html#r26)\] will have adverse consequences for the pharmaceutical business responsible for their production and any other entities associated with their distribution. Hence, it is essential to develop a robust pharmaceutical tracking system to combat counterfeit drugs. Nations throughout the globe are undertaking substantial endeavors to enhance drug monitoring systems. By implementing a secure drug traceability system, individuals such as patients and other relevant parties may conveniently monitor the whereabouts of medicine along the drug supply chain and verify its authenticity \[[27](#9789815305210-C8.html#r27)\]. The present medication monitoring systems lack essential components for the pharmaceutical supply chain. The existing healthcare system’s lack of transparency may potentially contribute to issues such as medicine shortages, opioid addiction, and the presence of counterfeit drugs. Due to the lack of transparency in the drug supply chain, it is difficult for patients and other stakeholders to monitor the whereabouts of their medications at any given moment. This issue is significant since the well-being of patients might be contingent upon the effectiveness of the treatments. By utilizing the resources and measures outlined in this treaty and protocol, we can effectively safeguard public health, halt the proliferation of counterfeit medication, and emerge victorious in our battle against them at national and local levels. We aim to establish a unified and comprehensive strategy to eradicate counterfeit tablets while promoting uniformity regarding definitions, information resources, and tools. This concerted effort aims to eliminate the problem.
